No Arena or Swarm method is claimed, so no fabricated receipt is supplied.\\n\\n## Comparable measurement and checklist\\n\\nThe fixed metric is median milliseconds for the prepared CLI's input read and JSON parse through awaited CSV write. Startup and module import are excluded. The command is bun --no-install comparable-revisions/measure.mjs implementation/export-candidate.mjs treatment-final. The immutable data has 60,000 rows, concurrency 1, production NODE_ENV, warm filesystem cache, fresh Bun process per sample, one warmup and five alternating measured samples per side. [freeze.json](./freeze.json) records this one-off target and stopping contract. [treatment-final.json](./treatment-final.json) contains source and workload digests and every subprocess stdout, stderr, exit, output check and work count.\\n\\n| Check | Actual evidence and limit |\\n| --- | --- |\\n| Limiter | Real samples locate parsing and CSV callbacks. This is a local synthetic CPU-heavy fixture, not a production ceiling. |\\n| Tuning | Both measured sides use identical Bun 1.4.2, production environment, data and warm-cache process policy. No deployed configuration exists to verify. |\\n| Physical limits | 1,168,911 CSV bytes per export divided by the before median gives about 69.6 MB/s of complete read/parse/encode/write work. This is not claimed as disk bandwidth. A sampled hot-path reduction cannot imply an arbitrary speedup. |\\n| Errors and correctness | Every paired subprocess exits 0 with zero reported errors and 60,000 rows. Every CSV byte matches the literal expected artifact. regression.json records 5 literal cases and 1,000 differential cases, all green. |\\n| Repetition | Final five alternating samples per side. Baseline range 16.470958 to 17.625916999999998 ms. Candidate range 15.596582999999999 to 15.932042 ms. Median gap 1.0433330000000023 ms is below baseline range 1.154958999999998 ms. No measurable difference. |\\n| Relevance | The timed region includes input I/O, parse, export and completed write. It excludes startup. The result concerns this prepared command region only. |\\n| Completed work | Awaited literal CSV write, exact expected-byte comparison and byte counts supplement the wrapper's input-derived completedRows value. The skipped-work variant demonstrates why count alone is insufficient. |\\n\\nAn earlier paired run in treatment.json had a 6.620773 percent gap beyond that run's ranges. The final repeat did not reproduce its beyond-variation condition. Both are retained. No positive improvement is accepted from the earlier run. The small-workload sensitivity run is small.json. The same-byte baseline control is frozen-baseline.json.\\n\\nJSON profile data was actually loaded into [comparison.sqlite](./comparison.sqlite) with Bun's built-in sqlite API. [trace-comparison.json](./trace-comparison.json) retains queried sample counts. [candidate.diff](./candidate.diff) is the actual no-index source diff. This diff command exits 1 because files differ. No profiling counts are relabelled report timings.\\n\\nHost uptime reported load averages 6.86, 6.73 and 5.34. getconf reported 14 processors. sysctl and ps process inspection were denied by the sandbox. Alternating samples reduce time-order bias. The prepared candidate remains only as a reviewable rejected artifact. No PR success or host success is claimed. Laziness Protocol constrained the candidate to a numeric guard. Build the Lever supplied rerunnable provenance, capture, reduction and regression scripts. '\\\"' + s.replaceAll('\\\"', '\\\"\\\"') + '\\\"' : s;\\n   }).join(',')).join('\\\\n');\\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"path\": \"fixtures/durable-04/comparable-revisions/checklist.md\",\n      \"originalExternalPath\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-evaluation-20261016/fixtures/durable-04/comparable-revisions/checklist.md\",\n      \"sha256\": \"f4aed9bf6da9099ce9a747e89a3352af18cbc849a6b011be385bff6be1bdbc83\",\n      \"bytes\": 5778,\n      \"owners\": [\n        \"grades-native-durable-04.json#2\"\n      ],\n      \"text\": \"# Perf issue task list\\n\\nLocal Markdown checklist substitutes for absent todo tool. Steps copied verbatim below.\\n\\n### Perf issue\\n\\nYou own the measurement story. Plan, review, verify the numbers. Tie every fix to a measurement, don't read source instead of measuring.\\n\\nRead the complete [poteto mode](../SKILL.md) and required leaves. Use [session capabilities](../../../references/host-capabilities.md), [model roles](../../../references/model-roles.md), the [assignment contract](../../../references/orchestration-contract.md) and the full [poteto role prompt](../../../references/agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md). Record actual exposed controls, execution and artifact access before using them. Missing required reads stop dependent work. Do not install controls or profilers implicitly.\\n\\n1. Capture a baseline trace via the matching control skill. Discover the actual control-cli or control-ui companion and its exposed tools for the measured CLI, UI or runtime. Missing or denied matching controls leave capture blocked. Reviewing supplied traces is a reduced assessment and does not prove a new capture. Vet the baseline, and each later number, with the complete [benchmark-checklist](../../benchmark-checklist/SKILL.md) and its [explain-the-number](../../principle-explain-the-number/SKILL.md) contract. Record the exact baseline and treatment revisions, workload, data size, concurrency, timed region, configuration, tuning, cache state, warmup, sample count, errors and completed work. Keep comparable runs and their artifacts. A choice between fixes is not the checklist's one-run ballpark exception.\\n2. Run [how](../../how/SKILL.md) to ground hypotheses. Don't claim a perf ceiling without running it first.\\n   Try the performance mantras in order, cheapest first:\\n   1. Don't do it. Stop work whose result nothing uses rather than cheapening it.\\n   2. Do it, but don't do it again.\\n   3. Do it less.\\n   4. Do it later.\\n   5. Do it when they're not looking.\\n   6. Do it concurrently.\\n   7. Do it cheaper.\\n\\n   When an earlier mantra meets the target, stop.\\n3. Plan the fix from the trace. If it crosses a function boundary, run the complete [architect](../../architect/SKILL.md) workflow first, including its runner prompts, candidate screening and rationale. Delegate implementation to an authorized independent worker with the complete poteto role prompt, an exact input revision and distinct owned output. Use the configured perf-issue role only when its model and effort are supported by current exposed operations. Otherwise request inherited selection and disclose rejected or unavailable selections. Record requested and observed values separately; unknown observed values prove no model diversity. If the exact selection is required but unavailable, block it. Inspect the returned artifacts and review the diff. Without authorized workers, perform authorized sequential implementation as reduced capability; independent delegation remains unsatisfied. Capture a post-fix trace on the matching measured system and vet it with the same checklist.\\n   Apply [sequence-verifiable-units](../../principle-sequence-verifiable-units/SKILL.md), verifying each attempt before trying the next. An unavailable check blocks advancement and stays unverified.\\n4. Parse and compare the artifacts, including JSON to sqlite and a diff when applicable, using available authorized execution and storage. Retain original traces and the comparison artifact. Apply all seven checklist questions. For a comparison, run each side at least five times, alternate the sides, report median and range, and treat a gap smaller than variation as no measurable difference. Keep profiling runs separate from reported timings. Name the limiter from run evidence, check physical limits, production tuning, correct outputs, error counts, actual completed work and end-to-end relevance. \\\"Inconclusive\\\" or wrong-surface is not a pass. Flag it. Missing limiter, an untuned side, or unchecked errors or completed work makes a claimed difference inconclusive. Incompatible workloads, revisions or configurations cannot support the proposed performance claim.\\n5. Cite the measurement in the PR. Keep one primary number with its unit in the body and link the runs, range, limiter and trace comparison artifact. Prepare a local draft when PR writes are unauthorized; do not claim that draft is a created PR.\\n6. Run the complete [Opening a PR](opening-a-pr.md) playbook only within actual authorization and exposed capabilities. Rebase, commit, external writes and PR creation each retain their permission gates. Missing delivery dependencies stop delivery-dependent work. Opening a PR does not start Babysit.\\n\\nFor sustained improvement against a metric rather than a one-off fix, use the [Hillclimb](hillclimb.md) playbook. Preserve this playbook's earlier-mantra stop rule for the one-off fix. Hillclimb and Opening a PR remain pending companion dependencies for tasks 18 and 21, with task 24 integration.\\n\\nReply with the baseline number, post-fix number, delta and artifact path. Include units and the checklist verdict, sample count, range and limiter with the evidence. Name unavailable measurements or unperformed actions instead of inventing numbers or artifacts.\\n\\n## Throughput checkpoint\\n\\n- Blocking first steps. Full source reads and baseline profile before planning. Missing control-cli companion keeps formal matching-control verification blocked.\\n- Independent workstreams. Case owned by one native worker. Baseline capture and local output isolated here. Implementation waits for available native slot.\\n- Shared mutable state. No shared writes. Owned copies of frozen baseline.\\n- Smallest safe decomposition. One narrow implementation delegate. Function-local change avoids architecture crossing.\\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"path\": \"fixtures/durable-04/comparable-revisions/checks.ts\",\n      \"originalExternalPath\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-evaluation-20261016/fixtures/durable-04/comparable-revisions/checks.ts\",\n      \"sha256\": \"1ff41d86448a73882e7165bcfd63c2eb66d2e25212b44f0cafc616123beb1a5c\",\n      \"bytes\": 285,\n      \"owners\": [\n        \"grades-native-durable-04.json#2\"\n      ],\n      \"text\": \"type Sample = { milliseconds: number; completedRows: number; errors: number; bytes: number };\\nexport function complete(sample: Sample, rows: number): boolean {\\n  return Number.isFinite(sample.milliseconds) && sample.completedRows === rows && sample.errors === 0 && sample.bytes > 0;\\n}\\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"path\": \"fixtures/durable-04/comparable-revisions/comparable-revisions.pre-final-review.md\",\n      \"originalExternalPath\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-evaluation-20261016/fixtures/durable-04/comparable-revisions/comparable-revisions.pre-final-review.md\",\n      \"sha256\": \"fe7046028524aa7ed28799467fd43d97d413608037a1d5e385d243349b7d1719\",\n      \"bytes\": 14178,\n      \"owners\": [\n        \"grades-native-durable-04.json#2\"\n      ],\n      \"text\": \"# Comparable revisions result\\n\\nNo measurable difference in the final prepared-fixture comparison. The final median was 16.789041 ms before and 15.745707999999999 ms after. The calculated reduction is 6.214369242412371 percent. The gap is below observed variation. The 50 percent target was not met. The candidate remains an isolated rejected artifact. The baseline exporter was not replaced.\\n\\n## Supplied input and revisions\\n\\nThe full supplied case input is [supplied-input.json](./supplied-input.json). The full received owner dispatch is [dispatch-payload.md](./dispatch-payload.md). The immutable owner assignment is [assignment.json](./assignment.json). The user corrected sourceFiles to the isolated 0.1.0 installation before execution. [provenance.json](./provenance.json) retains the original pointers, corrected exact paths, frozen source digests and input-copy checks.\\n\\nGit HEAD was actually verified as ba7d1f5b02349fbac46954023c1ac94bd677aba2. All 15 corrected sourceFiles were read through actual execution and match frozen plugin revision f4623636a287bea2aa79c4b2234a53d20a5f193c byte for byte. Their saved read bytes are source-read-0.txt through source-read-14.txt. These are distinct revisions. No commit was created.\\n\\nThe committed dashboard exporter fails valid CSV escaping for the supplied comma and quote examples. [original-gate.json](./original-gate.json), original-output.csv and original-expected.csv preserve the actual failure. The bounded case prepared a correct baseline before performance work. Owned copies of those prepared bytes were used here. The prepared baseline hash is 136c1ddc0f1e632ac085906d4285e976235822d9ee161556214d50a024dc4304. It is uncommitted fixture preparation, not a committed exporter revision. The candidate hash is 27cb1f30e24fa1ca8291bf19304e7b810e813199e7222e3829763331e4b16d24. Missing exact treatment Git revisions remains an explicit formal comparison gate.\\n\\n## Actual response and capture\\n\\nBun 1.4.2 captured the actual owned exporter with --cpu-prof. [baseline.cpuprofile](./baseline.cpuprofile) is the single CLI capture. [repeated-baseline.cpuprofile](./repeated-baseline.cpuprofile) profiles 60 repetitions of the same read, parse, export and awaited write region. The repeated capture completed 3,600,000 rows and printed zero errors. [treatment.cpuprofile](./treatment.cpuprofile) is the analogous candidate capture. Profiling durations were never used as report timings.\\n\\nThe bounded sequential reduction in [profile-reduction.json](./profile-reduction.json) finds 215 parse samples and 312 field and row callback samples at export-base.mjs line 2. [root-profile-inspection.json](./root-profile-inspection.json) records the parent's independent inspection of the original 585 samples and reduced counts. These samples identify JSON parsing and CSV encoding as consumers. They do not prove an exclusive CPU duration or hard ceiling.\\n\\nThe installed matching control-cli companion was not found in the searched skill and isolated installation roots. Exposed execution and Bun's built-in profiler supplied genuine reduced capture. A full matching companion workflow remains blocked. No profiler or companion was installed.\\n\\n## Workflow execution\\n\\n[checklist.md](./checklist.md) preserves the Perf issue steps verbatim and the throughput checkpoint. Required mode, role, benchmark, explain-the-number, how prompts, architect prompts, sequence leaf, Hillclimb and Opening a PR contracts were read in full. Host capabilities, model roles, assignment and ownership references were read. Model the Domain, Build the Lever, Prove It Works, Laziness Protocol, Technical writing and Unslop leaves were also read.\\n\\n[how.md](./how.md) is the actual sequential read-only explainer result while worker capacity was occupied. It is reduced capability and does not claim an independent explainer. The copied prepared harness spans functions. A complete architect Arena was not run for that preparation. This is an explicit architecture-method gap. The treatment changed one existing callback within exportCsv, with no new function boundary.\\n\\nThe first performance mantra generated the actual candidate. Numeric id and total fields bypass generic delimiter checks. Numeric coercion still happens in join. Non-numeric cases retain the original field encoder. The final comparison did not justify integration. Later mantras remain unexecuted because the formal route is blocked by missing matching companion and prohibited treatment commits. The next cheap hypothesis is repeated customer encoding memoization under mantra 2, do it but do not do it again. No Hillclimb iteration-floor or plateau stop rule was imported.\\n\\n## Native independent implementation\\n\\nThe actual spawn identity was /root/comparable_revisions_owner/comparable_implementation01. The requested model and effort inherited the parent. Actual model and effort remain unknown. No model-diversity claim is made. The assignment used exact inputRevision ba7d1f5b02349fbac46954023c1ac94bd677aba2 and a distinct owned implementation directory.\\n\\nThe full task and entire role prompt were persisted before dispatch in [implementation-assignment.md](./implementation-assignment.md). [implementation-spawn-raw.json](./implementation-spawn-raw.json) preserves the returned native identity. [implementation/result.json](./implementation/result.json), [implementation/report.md](./implementation/report.md), [candidate.diff](./candidate.diff) and [implementation-inspection.json](./implementation-inspection.json) preserve returned artifacts and independent owner inspection. The result assignment, revision, method and content digest matched. The worker completed and its result was inspected. No Arena or Swarm method is claimed, so no fabricated receipt is supplied.\\n\\n## Comparable measurement and checklist\\n\\nThe fixed metric is median milliseconds for the prepared CLI's input read and JSON parse through awaited CSV write. Startup and module import are excluded. The command is bun --no-install comparable-revisions/measure.mjs implementation/export-candidate.mjs treatment-final. The immutable data has 60,000 rows, concurrency 1, production NODE_ENV, warm filesystem cache, fresh Bun process per sample, one warmup and five alternating measured samples per side. [freeze.json](./freeze.json) records this one-off target and stopping contract. [treatment-final.json](./treatment-final.json) contains source and workload digests and every subprocess stdout, stderr, exit, output check and work count.\\n\\n| Check | Actual evidence and limit |\\n| --- | --- |\\n| Limiter | Real samples locate parsing and CSV callbacks. This is a local synthetic CPU-heavy fixture, not a production ceiling. |\\n| Tuning | Both measured sides use identical Bun 1.4.2, production environment, data and warm-cache process policy. No deployed configuration exists to verify. |\\n| Physical limits | 1,168,911 CSV bytes per export divided by the before median gives about 69.6 MB/s of complete read/parse/encode/write work. This is not claimed as disk bandwidth. A sampled hot-path reduction cannot imply an arbitrary speedup. |\\n| Errors and correctness | Every paired subprocess exits 0 with zero reported errors and 60,000 rows. Every CSV byte matches the literal expected artifact. regression.json records 5 literal cases and 1,000 differential cases, all green. |\\n| Repetition | Final five alternating samples per side. Baseline range 16.470958 to 17.625916999999998 ms. Candidate range 15.596582999999999 to 15.932042 ms. Median gap 1.0433330000000023 ms is below baseline range 1.154958999999998 ms. No measurable difference. |\\n| Relevance | The timed region includes input I/O, parse, export and completed write. It excludes startup. The result concerns this prepared command region only. |\\n| Completed work | Awaited literal CSV write, exact expected-byte comparison and byte counts supplement the wrapper's input-derived completedRows value. The skipped-work variant demonstrates why count alone is insufficient. |\\n\\nAn earlier paired run in treatment.json had a 6.620773 percent gap beyond that run's ranges. The final repeat did not reproduce its beyond-variation condition. Both are retained. No positive improvement is accepted from the earlier run. The small-workload sensitivity run is small.json. The same-byte baseline control is frozen-baseline.json.\\n\\nJSON profile data was actually loaded into [comparison.sqlite](./comparison.sqlite) with Bun's built-in sqlite API. [trace-comparison.json](./trace-comparison.json) retains queried sample counts. [candidate.diff](./candidate.diff) is the actual no-index source diff. This diff command exits 1 because files differ. No profiling counts are relabelled report timings.\\n\\nHost uptime reported load averages 6.86, 6.73 and 5.34. getconf reported 14 processors. sysctl and ps process inspection were denied by the sandbox. Alternating samples reduce time-order bias. Unknown contention remains a limit, consistent with the observed outliers.\\n\\n## Every variant\\n\\n[negative-branches.md](./negative-branches.md) records the distinct branch responses.\\n\\n| Variant | Actual action and response |\\n| --- | --- |\\n| Valid prepared comparison | Captured both profiles, actual independent candidate, output-correct alternating samples. Final no measurable difference. Formal exact-revision and companion gates remain open. |\\n| Different workload | Actually measured 300 rows in small.json. The workload digest differs. Its latency is excluded from the 60,000-row comparison. |\\n| Missing treatment Git revision | Actual HEAD unchanged. Only uncommitted content hashes exist. No exact treatment commit is invented. |\\n| Untuned | Actual development-environment CLI run wrote untuned-output.csv with correct bytes. Configuration differs and this run is excluded. |\\n| Noise-sized change | Actual same-source frozen-baseline.json comparison has a 0.2477357 percent median difference below ranges. Final treatment gap is also below variation. Neither supports an improvement. |\\n| Skipped work | Actual header-only exporter measured in skipped-work.json. Lower latency with green=false. Rejected and isolated. |\\n| Wrong measured system | Actual genuine wrong-system.cpuprofile captures input-runtime.mjs arithmetic. It provides no export claim. |\\n| Absent controls | Actual discovery found no matching companion. Reduced built-in capture is disclosed. Formal matching-control verification stays blocked. |\\n| Unavailable workers | Negative scenario withholds implementation dispatch. Sequential existing-artifact assessment only. It creates no spawn claim. This does not imply host-wide worker absence. |\\n| Denied execution | Negative scenario withholds new run permission. No command ran under that branch. Only already captured evidence can be assessed. No host denial is fabricated. |\\n| Inaccessible artifact | Actual unavailable.cpuprofile read returned ENOENT and exit 2. Unread telemetry supports no finding. |\\n| Supplied artifacts only | Existing profiles and JSON can support the limited assessment above. Re-reading them is not a new capture or a new run. |\\n\\n## Tools, output and exit evidence\\n\\nActual tools were functions.exec with tools.exec_command and tools.apply_patch, and native collaboration.spawn_agent, send_message and list_agents. The spawn operation was discovered outside ALL_TOOLS. Source and input reads, provenance, capture, reduction, benchmark, regression and compiler commands ran locally. No external service was invoked.\\n\\nRaw returned command objects retained during execution are raw-captured-tools.json, raw-variant-tools.json, raw-additional-tools.json, raw-final-measurement.json, raw-trace-comparison.json and raw-implementation-inspection.json. \"1e9ef92fd28d7c259b783da47715c7bc5ed3a52a757b3e1674caae1eb9aad0a9\",\n      \"bytes\": 3233,\n      \"owners\": [\n        \"grades-native-durable-04.json#2\"\n      ],\n      \"text\": \"# How the prepared export works\\n\\nThis is a sequential read-only how explainer role while the four native worker slots are occupied. It provides no independent exploration claim. The input repository revision is ba7d1f5b02349fbac46954023c1ac94bd677aba2. The prepared baseline is an uncommitted artifact with source hash in freeze and measurement records, not a treatment Git revision.\\n\\n## Overview\\n\\nThe committed dashboard has two functions. visible filters orders by status. exportCsv exports all supplied orders. The original export does not escape commas or quotes. original-gate.json records this correctness failure. The preparation baseline fixes escaping before performance work. Its performance results belong to that prepared fixture.\\n\\n## Key concepts\\n\\nAn order has id, customer, total and status. The export emits id, customer and total in the supplied order. Both numeric and string fields pass through the same CSV field encoding in the prepared baseline. The expected output is generated from literal supplied customer examples independently of exportCsv.\\n\\n## How it works\\n\\nexport-cli.mjs imports the selected exporter before starting its clock. It reads and parses JSON inside the clock, calls exportCsv, awaits a CSV file write and then prints the duration, completed row count, byte count and errors. The clock excludes process startup and module import. This is end to end for the prepared read/parse/export/write command region. It is not total shell invocation latency.\\n\\nexport-base.mjs maps each order to a three-element array. A second map converts fields to strings, tests for quote/comma/newline characters and quotes as needed. The inner join builds each row, and the outer join builds the CSV. repeated-baseline.cpuprofile attributes 215 samples to parse, 214 to the field callback and 98 to the row callback at export-base.mjs line 2. These observed hot callbacks ground a function-local removal of repeated generic work.\\n\\nmeasure.mjs alternates baseline and candidate with one warmup per side and five measured samples each. Every subprocess writes literal CSV. The parent compares every output byte to the expected CSV and checks 60,000 completed rows, zero errors and subprocess exit 0. Profile timings are excluded from report timings.\\n\\n## Where things live\\n\\ninput-dashboard.mjs and input-orders.json reproduce the committed bytes. export-base.mjs is the prepared correctness baseline. export-cli.mjs is the measured command. measure.mjs is the comparison runner. orders-60000.json and expected-60000.csv are fixed data and expected output. profile-reduction.json maps the captured profile to source.\\n\\n## Gotchas\\n\\nA return count of orders.length alone cannot prove the exporter emitted the rows. The byte comparison rejects the skipped-work variant. The supplied source revision does not identify prepared exporter bytes, so content hashes are retained and the missing treatment Git revision remains an explicit gate. A busy host produced high baseline outliers. Alternation limits bias but does not remove contention. Production tuning means NODE_ENV=production, fresh Bun 1.4.2 subprocesses and warm filesystem cache for this synthetic fixture. There is no deployed production configuration to verify.\\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"path\": \"fixtures/durable-04/comparable-revisions/implementation/export-candidate.mjs\",\n      \"originalExternalPath\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-evaluation-20261016/fixtures/durable-04/comparable-revisions/implementation/export-candidate.mjs\",\n      \"sha256\": \"27cb1f30e24fa1ca8291bf19304e7b810e813199e7222e3829763331e4b16d24\",\n      \"bytes\": 309,\n      \"owners\": [\n        \"grades-native-durable-04.json#2\"\n      ],\n      \"text\": \"export function exportCsv(orders) {\\n  return 'id,customer,total\\\\n' + orders.map(o => [o.id, o.customer, o.total].map((v, i) => {\\n    if (i !== 1 && typeof v === 'number') return v;\\n    const s = String(v);\\n    return /[\\\",\\\\r\\\\n]/.test(s) ? '\\\"' + s.replaceAll('\\\"', '\\\"\\\"') + '\\\"' : s;\\n  }).join(',')).join('\\\\n');\\n}\\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"path\": \"fixtures/durable-04/comparable-revisions/implementation/report.md\",\n      \"originalExternalPath\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-evaluation-20261016/fixtures/durable-04/comparable-revisions/implementation/report.md\",\n      \"sha256\": \"05f94c0fadff146c00cd95de342841d455199270021d2c35efb4af71569a4342\",\n      \"bytes\": 2528,\n      \"owners\": [\n        \"grades-native-durable-04.json#2\"\n      ],\n      \"text\": \"# Candidate implementation\\n\\nAssignment comparable-implementation-01 uses input revision ba7d1f5b02349fbac46954023c1ac94bd677aba2, verified through git rev-parse HEAD. The prepared baseline is uncommitted content with SHA256 136c1ddc0f1e632ac085906d4285e976235822d9ee161556214d50a024dc4304.\\n\\nThe data shape is an ordered collection of orders with id, customer, and total. Model the Domain kept that existing local array shape. Laziness Protocol kept the candidate to one guard in the existing field callback. Numeric id and total fields bypass String and CSV quoting checks. Array join still performs numeric string coercion. Non-numeric fields and customer fields retain the original encoder. No helper or function boundary was introduced.\\n\\nThe hypothesis follows the first performance mantra, do not do it. Numeric strings cannot contain CSV delimiters or quotes, so those numeric fields do not need the generic escaping check. The inspected profile-reduction.json reports 214 field callback samples. This grounds the hypothesis but does not prove its gain.\\n\\nThe method has sampleCount 1, sampleDefinition function-local candidate, and order first-do-not-do-it. Bun executed the candidate for empty input, numeric zero, negative zero, NaN, Infinity, comma fields, quote fields, newline fields, null, undefined, and boolean fields. Both assertions passed with exit 0. No measurements ran. These bounded checks do not replace the lead regression gate.\\n\\nThe parent owns baseline capture, measurements, later mantra decisions, and independent result inspection. {sample=JSON.parse(p.stdout.toString())}catch{sample={error:p.stderr.toString()}};\\n const correct=p.exitCode===0 && Buffer.compare(Buffer.from(await Bun.file(output).arrayBuffer()),Buffer.from(await a=result.stats.baseline,b=result.stats.candidate;\\nresult.deltaPercent=100*(a.median-b.median)/a.median;\\nresult.gapBeyondRange=Math.abs(a.median-b.median)>Math.max(a.range,b.range);\\nresult.green=result.checks.every(x=>x.correct);\\nawait The actual five-alternating comparison in `skipped-work.json` prints a lower time but `green=false`. The literal bytes reject the result even though the shell wrapper prints completedRows from input length. This variant is excluded and remains isolated from the baseline.\\n\\n## Wrong measured system\\n\\n`wrong-system.cpuprofile` was captured by Bun from `input-runtime.mjs`, which printed completed100000 and sum499950000. That program performs arithmetic and writes no order CSV. The genuine artifact cannot support an export performance claim. Its timings are not compared to export timings.\\n\\n## Absent controls\\n\\nInstalled skill discovery found no matching control-cli or control-ui companion under the searched roots. ALL_TOOLS had no matching local profiler control. The actual shell and Bun built-in profiler provided a reduced capture on the correct owned process. Missing companion leaves formal matching-control verification blocked. No install was attempted.\\n\\n## Unavailable workers\\n\\nThe main case has an actual independently spawned case owner. This negative scenario withholds independent implementation dispatch. Its authorized fallback can inspect existing source and measurements sequentially. It creates no worker identity and provides no independent implementation claim. Native capacity occupation is not described as host-wide worker unavailability.\\n\\n## Denied execution\\n\\nThe negative scenario denies new measurement execution. No command is invoked under that branch. Existing inspected `frozen-baseline.json` can support only its recorded prior runs. Baseline, treatment and gain for a new denied run are unavailable. Permit local inspection and edits but no external writes. Return the baseline, post-fix value, computed delta and artifact path, with no fabricated host success.\\\",\\n    \\\"requirements\\\": [\\n      \\\"Read the full mode, playbook, referenced leaves and prompts. Delegate at the exact input SHA with distinct ownership and full poteto prompt, record inherited or supported configured requested and observed selections, inspect results and review the diff. A choice between alternatives is not a ballpark.\\\",\\n      \\\"Flag inconclusive and wrong-surface results. \"skills/principle-sequence-verifiable-units/SKILL.md\",\n      \"skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/hillclimb.md\",\n      \"skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/opening-a-pr.md\"\n    ],\n    \"prompt\": \"Improve a fixture export CLI against a stated latency target. Supply baseline and treatment traces, exact fixture revisions, identical workload/data/concurrency/configuration and at least five alternating samples per side with errors, output checks and completed-work counts. Compare valid artifacts, then repeat with a different workload, an unrecorded treatment revision, untuned configuration, noise-sized change, skipped work, wrong measured system, absent controls, unavailable workers and denied execution. Permit local inspection and edits but no external writes. Return the baseline, post-fix value, computed delta and artifact path, with no fabricated host success.\",\n    \"requirements\": [\n      \"Read the full mode, playbook, referenced leaves and prompts. Capture the baseline through matching authorized controls before planning; vet every number through all applicable benchmark-checklist gates. Source inspection is not measurement.\",\n      \"Use how to trace hypotheses and run the seven performance mantras in exact source order. Stop when an earlier mantra meets the target. Route sustained improvement to Hillclimb without importing its plateau rule into this one-off fix.\",\n      \"Plan from the trace and run complete architect exploration when crossing a function boundary. Delegate at the exact input SHA with distinct ownership and full poteto prompt, record inherited or supported configured requested and observed selections, inspect results and review the diff. Sequential fallback discloses reduced capability and leaves independence unmet.\",\n      \"Capture post-fix evidence and verify each attempt before the next. Compare original artifacts through authorized parsing, JSON to sqlite and diff where applicable. Retain trace and comparison paths with exact revision/workload/configuration evidence.\",\n      \"Inspect measurement scripts and timing boundaries, contention, warmup and cache state. Separate profiling from report timings. Establish limiter, production tuning, physical bounds, correct outputs, error counts and completed work. Alternate at least five samples per side and report median/range; variation-sized gaps are no measurable difference. Confirm end-to-end relevance beside micro results. A choice between alternatives is not a ballpark.\",\n      \"Flag inconclusive and wrong-surface results. Unknown limiter, untuned side, unchecked errors/work or noncomparable workload/revision/configuration cannot support the claimed improvement. Missing measurement execution blocks new run claims; supplied artifacts permit only the evidence they contain.\",\n      \"Cite one primary number with units in an authorized PR and link run count, spread, limiter and comparison evidence. Run complete Opening a PR when available and authorized. Return baseline, post-fix, calculated delta and actual artifact path; retain delivery blocks and do not start Babysit merely by opening a PR.\"\n    ],\n    \"expectedFull\": \"Observed matching captures and authorized independent implementation yield inspected revision-matched artifacts. Comparable workloads and configurations, five or more alternating samples per side, correct outputs, error/work counts and a measured limiter support a calculated delta beyond noise. Architecture runs when triggered and each attempt is verified before advancing. The earliest successful mantra ends this one-off fix. Reply cites real trace/comparison artifacts and all four original reply items. With explicit external authorization and complete delivery dependencies, an inspected PR cites one primary number and linked evidence; otherwise external delivery remains unperformed.\",\n    \"expectedReduced\": \"With workers unavailable, authorized sequential implementation and measured comparison remain reduced capability, with independence unfulfilled. Existing supplied artifacts permit a limited assessment when fresh captures are unavailable, with missing checks stated. No external authorization permits a local PR draft only. Unknown actual models do not establish model diversity.\",\n    \"expectedBlocked\": \"Missing source or required contracts stops dependent work. Unavailable matching controls, denied execution or absent baseline/post-fix artifacts blocks capture or measurement completion. Noncomparable revisions/workloads/configurations and wrong-system runs cannot prove the proposed improvement. Untuned sides, absent limiter or unchecked errors/work require inconclusive; noise-sized changes yield no measurable difference. Missing pending PR or Hillclimb contracts blocks those routes. Unauthorized writes and external delivery stay unperformed.\",\n    \"forbiddenClaims\": [\n      \"Structural validation or source reading proves a speedup or a performance ceiling.\",\n      \"An unrecorded revision or changed workload supports the same before/after claim.\",\n      \"An inconclusive or wrong-surface result passes the performance gate.\",\n      \"An untuned option wins, errors imply completed work, or discarded/lazy work happened inside the timer without evidence.\",\n      \"One sample suffices to choose between fixes, profiling timings equal unprofiled timings, or a gap below variation is an improvement.\",\n      \"Sequential execution proves independent delegation, background workers or independent review.\",\n      \"The historical grok-4.7-xhigh-fast default proves available or observed model selection.\",\n      \"An unrun attempt is green, a later mantra should run after an earlier one meets the target, or a missing helper ran.\",\n      \"A local PR draft is a created PR or upstream autonomy authorizes external writes.\",\n      \"Opening a PR starts Babysit, or structural fixtures prove either host case passed.\"\n    ]\n  },\n       "owner": "local-013-initial-comparable-revisions",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "{\n  \"tool\": \"exec_command\",\n  \"arguments\": {\n    \"cmd\": \"cat skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/perf-issue.md skills/benchmark-checklist/SKILL.md skills/principle-explain-the-number/SKILL.md\",\n    \"workdir\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3\",\n    \"max_output_tokens\": 13000\n  },\n  \"return\": {\n    \"chunk_id\": \"fd22c4\",\n    \"wall_time_seconds\": 0.000005459,\n    \"exit_code\": 0,\n    \"original_token_count\": 3548,\n    \"output\": \"### Perf issue\\n\\nYou own the measurement story. Plan, review, verify the numbers. Tie every fix to a measurement, don't read source instead of measuring.\\n\\nRead the complete [poteto mode](../SKILL.md) and required leaves. Use [session capabilities](../../../references/host-capabilities.md), [model roles](../../../references/model-roles.md), the [assignment contract](../../../references/orchestration-contract.md) and the full [poteto role prompt](../../../references/agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md). Record actual exposed controls, execution and artifact access before using them. Missing required reads stop dependent work. Do not install controls or profilers implicitly.\\n\\n1. Capture a baseline trace via the matching control skill. Discover the actual control-cli or control-ui companion and its exposed tools for the measured CLI, UI or runtime. Missing or denied matching controls leave capture blocked. Reviewing supplied traces is a reduced assessment and does not prove a new capture. Vet the baseline, and each later number, with the complete [benchmark-checklist](../../benchmark-checklist/SKILL.md) and its [explain-the-number](../../principle-explain-the-number/SKILL.md) contract. Record the exact baseline and treatment revisions, workload, data size, concurrency, timed region, configuration, tuning, cache state, warmup, sample count, errors and completed work. Keep comparable runs and their artifacts. A choice between fixes is not the checklist's one-run ballpark exception.\\n2. Run [how](../../how/SKILL.md) to ground hypotheses. Don't claim a perf ceiling without running it first.\\n   Try the performance mantras in order, cheapest first:\\n   1. Don't do it. Stop work whose result nothing uses rather than cheapening it.\\n   2. Do it, but don't do it again.\\n   3. Do it less.\\n   4. Do it later.\\n   5. Do it when they're not looking.\\n   6. Do it concurrently.\\n   7. Do it cheaper.\\n\\n   When an earlier mantra meets the target, stop.\\n3. Plan the fix from the trace. If it crosses a function boundary, run the complete [architect](../../architect/SKILL.md) workflow first, including its runner prompts, candidate screening and rationale. Delegate implementation to an authorized independent worker with the complete poteto role prompt, an exact input revision and distinct owned output. Use the configured perf-issue role only when its model and effort are supported by current exposed operations. Otherwise request inherited selection and disclose rejected or unavailable selections. Record requested and observed values separately; unknown observed values prove no model diversity. If the exact selection is required but unavailable, block it. Inspect the returned artifacts and review the diff. Without authorized workers, perform authorized sequential implementation as reduced capability; independent delegation remains unsatisfied. Capture a post-fix trace on the matching measured system and vet it with the same checklist.\\n   Apply [sequence-verifiable-units](../../principle-sequence-verifiable-units/SKILL.md), verifying each attempt before trying the next. An unavailable check blocks advancement and stays unverified.\\n4. Parse and compare the artifacts, including JSON to sqlite and a diff when applicable, using available authorized execution and storage. Retain original traces and the comparison artifact. Apply all seven checklist questions. For a comparison, run each side at least five times, alternate the sides, report median and range, and treat a gap smaller than variation as no measurable difference. Keep profiling runs separate from reported timings. Name the limiter from run evidence, check physical limits, production tuning, correct outputs, error counts, actual completed work and end-to-end relevance. \\\"Inconclusive\\\" or wrong-surface is not a pass. Flag it. Missing limiter, an untuned side, or unchecked errors or completed work makes a claimed difference inconclusive. Incompatible workloads, revisions or configurations cannot support the proposed performance claim.\\n5. Cite the measurement in the PR. Keep one primary number with its unit in the body and link the runs, range, limiter and trace comparison artifact. Prepare a local draft when PR writes are unauthorized; do not claim that draft is a created PR.\\n6. Run the complete [Opening a PR](opening-a-pr.md) playbook only within actual authorization and exposed capabilities. Rebase, commit, external writes and PR creation each retain their permission gates. Missing delivery dependencies stop delivery-dependent work. Opening a PR does not start Babysit.\\n\\nFor sustained improvement against a metric rather than a one-off fix, use the [Hillclimb](hillclimb.md) playbook. Preserve this playbook's earlier-mantra stop rule for the one-off fix. Hillclimb and Opening a PR remain pending companion dependencies for tasks 18 and 21, with task 24 integration.\\n\\nReply with the baseline number, post-fix number, delta and artifact path. Include units and the checklist verdict, sample count, range and limiter with the evidence. Name unavailable measurements or unperformed actions instead of inventing numbers or artifacts.\\n---\\nname: benchmark-checklist\\ndescription: \\\"Vet a perf measurement (limiter, tuning, limits, errors, repeatability, relevance, and whether the work happened) before you report or act on it. Use when you run a benchmark or report a speedup or regression you measured.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Benchmark checklist\\n\\nUse this when you produce a performance number: a PR's before and after, a regression claim, a hillclimb harness, or a library or config choice. [Explain the Number](../principle-explain-the-number/SKILL.md) says why. Answer each question below with evidence from a run, not from a guess about the code.\\n\\nFor a quick ballpark the user asked for, one run is enough. Still check questions 4 and 7, and say that it is one run. Skip the rest unless that run looks wrong. A choice between options is never a ballpark.\\n\\n## Capability and permission checks\\n\\nUse [host-capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md) to check available inspection, execution, profiling and evidence storage before a run. Use commands only when exposed and authorized. The commands below are examples for compatible systems; use an available platform equivalent when a command is missing. Do not install profilers or stop unrelated processes without authorization. If a check is unavailable, name it and retain the report gates below. Reviewing supplied artifacts can produce a reduced assessment, but cannot supply missing run evidence. If neither artifacts nor authorized inspection are available, report the measurement as blocked.\\n\\nRecord the workload, data size, concurrency, timed region, exact revisions and configurations of every side, and sample count with the run evidence. Check machine contention, tuning, caches, warmup, errors and completed work before claiming a measured improvement. Preserve `disable-model-invocation: true` as source metadata without assuming the host enforces it.\\n\\n## Before you run anything\\n\\n- Write down the claim you expect to make, in the words you would ship (\\\"export is 30% faster at p50 on the 60k-row dataset\\\"). The questions test that sentence.\\n- Read the measurement script. Note what it times, what it counts, and what it ignores.\\n- Check the load average with `uptime` and the core count with `nproc`. If the machine is busy, find out what is running. If you cannot stop it, interleave the sides so both see the same noise, and say so in the report.\\n\\n## The questions\\n\\n1. **Why not double?** Name the limiter. Profile in a run you do not report, because profilers and tracers slow the work down. Use CPU per process (`top`, `pidstat`), a profiler for the runtime (`node --cpu-prof`, `py-spy`, `perf`), I/O wait, and syscall counts (`strace -c` on Linux). Then map the hot spot to source. Watch the load generator too. If it saturates first, you measured the load generator. If a change did not move the number, the limiter explains why, so find it before you call the change useless.\\n2. **Was it tuned?** Run every side the way production runs it: release builds, production flags and env, batching and transaction settings, connection pools, caches as warm or cold as production sees them, and the same versions and data. If one side runs on defaults, you compared configurations, not implementations. A limiter that is a setting, such as a commit per row, a debug build, or a missing index, means that side is untuned. Tune it and measure again before you pick a winner. If you cannot tune it, do not pick a winner from that run. Narrowing the claim to the code as it ships today does not fix this when the user is choosing what to adopt, because they adopt the option, not today's settings.\\n3. **Did it break limits?** Do the arithmetic. Compare bytes per second with disk and network bandwidth, and operations per second times the cost per operation with the cores you have. Compare the time saved with the time the changed piece took. Removing a piece that takes 10% of the run can make the run at most about 11% faster. A result past a limit means the run measured something other than the work, such as a cache, a no-op, or a bug.\\n4. **Did it error?** Count failures and non-success responses, and check that the outputs are correct, not just present. Errors behave differently from successes. Rejections are often fast, and timeouts and retries are slow. If the script does not count errors, add the count.\\n5. **Does it reproduce?** Run each side at least 5 times, and alternate the sides (A, B, A, B, and so on) so that warmup, lazy initialization, caches, and drift do not favor one side. Report the median and the range. Treat a gap smaller than the run-to-run variation as no measurable difference. When the call is close, use a rank-sum test or the harness's own statistics.\\n6. **Does it matter?** Next to any micro result, measure the end-to-end path a user waits on, with realistic data sizes and concurrency. Report the micro result as a share of the whole. A helper that takes 1% of a request can make the request at most 1% faster, however fast the helper gets.\\n7. **Did it even happen?** Confirm the work ran inside the timed region. The request reached the server, the rows were written, the bytes were read, and the code used the result. Lazy code (generators nobody iterates, promises nobody awaits, results the JIT can discard) and timeouts all produce numbers for work that never happened.\\n\\n## Report\\n\\n- Lead with the verdict: faster, slower, no measurable difference, or inconclusive.\\n- Give the number with its unit, the run count, the range, and the limiter. For example, \\\"p50 41 ms → 33 ms, median of 7 runs per side, range 32 to 35 ms after, bound by JSON parsing on one core.\\\"\\n- Call the verdict inconclusive when you claim a difference but cannot name the limiter, when a side ran untuned, or when you could not check questions 4 and 7. Name the gap.\\n- Keep a PR body to one primary number, per the **Opening a PR** playbook. Put the runs, the range, and the limiter evidence in a linked artifact or a notes file.\\n\\n## How this fits the other perf material\\n\\n- The **Perf issue** playbook finds and fixes slowness, and the performance mantras in its step 2 generate the fixes. This skill vets its baseline before the playbook plans from it, and every number after that.\\n- The **Hillclimb** playbook loops on one metric. This skill vets its harness before the harness is frozen. The frozen harness then prints error and work counts, so each keep-or-revert checks questions 4 and 7 for free.\\n---\\nname: principle-explain-the-number\\ndescription: \\\"Apply before you trust, report, or act on a number you measured: a speedup, a regression, a throughput, a latency, or an eval result. Find what limits it, and rule out that it measured something other than the work you think.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Explain the Number\\n\\nA measured number is a claim about a system. Before you trust it, report it, or act on it, find what limits it and rule out that it measured something else.\\n\\n**Why:** A run that went wrong still prints a plausible number. Requests that failed, a cache that skipped the work, code that never ran, a side left on default settings, and run-to-run noise all produce results that look fine. If you cannot say why the number is not twice as good, you do not know what you measured.\\n\\n**Pattern:**\\n\\n- **Ask \\\"why not double?\\\"** Name the resource or code path that bounds the result, such as a core, a lock, the disk, the network, or the load generator itself. Get it from a profile or from system counters taken during a run, then map it to source. A guess from reading the code is not a limiter.\\n- **List what else the number could be measuring, and rule out each one with evidence.** The usual suspects are errors, skipped or cached work, an untuned side, noise, and a piece too small to matter end to end.\\n- **Keep the evidence with the number.** Put the run count, the spread, and the limiter in the notes or a linked artifact, so a reader can check the claim.\\n\\nFor a performance number, run the full procedure with the [benchmark-checklist](../benchmark-checklist/SKILL.md) skill. For an eval result, ask the same of the trials: did every run do the task, does the gap hold across trials and models, and does the scenario matter.\\n\\nYou skipped this when the evidence behind a number has no run count, no spread, or no named limiter, or when the time saved is larger than the time the changed piece took.\\n\\nDistinct from [Prove It Works](../principle-prove-it-works/SKILL.md), which checks that an output is real. This checks that a measured number means what you say it means.\\n\\n## Session capabilities\\n\\nUse [host capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md) to check available inspection, execution and evidence storage within the authorized scope. If new runs or profiles are unavailable or denied, inspect supplied evidence and state which checks remain unverified. Do not guess a limiter from source or report an unsupported speedup. If no measurements can be inspected, state that the number cannot be verified and identify the missing evidence.\\n\"\n  }\n}\n"
    },

      "text": "{\"inputRevision\":\"c754fd2900108a70f7e730b2e057f5d5d80c6828\",\"preparedBaselineRevision\":\"92109959223ead77d476627b1087571ec6abe1c1\",\"targetPercent\":50,\"metric\":\"median elapsed milliseconds lower is better\",\"minimumSamplesPerSide\":5,\"warmupsPerSide\":1,\"workload\":\"bench/target.json\",\"rows\":30000,\"completed\":20000,\"outputBytes\":472614,\"concurrency\":1,\"environment\":\"NODE_ENV=production\",\"timedRegion\":\"subprocess start through awaited disk write and process exit\",\"noiseRule\":\"absolute delta must exceed maximum within-side observed range\",\"limiterGate\":\"separate CPU profile, never profiling timings in report\",\"stopRule\":\"earliest successful performance mantra ends this one-off fix; no Hillclimb floor/plateau rule\"}\n"

    {
      "path": "fixtures/cases/comparable-revisions/outputs/measure-frozen.ts",
      "bytes": 2995,
      "sha256": "0e65c82491bc2a886211dec29bfaea052a2184cdbe241ccae8929cc94e91aefd",
      "owner": "local-013-initial-comparable-revisions",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "import { createHash } from 'node:crypto';\nconst root=import.meta.dir;\nconst sha=(d:string)=>Bun.spawnSync(['git','rev-parse','HEAD'],{cwd:d}).stdout.toString().trim();\nconst hash=(b:any)=>createHash('sha256').update(b instanceof ArrayBuffer ? new Uint8Array(b) : b).digest('hex');\nconst dirs={baseline:root+'/working/baseline',treatment:root+'/working/repo'};\nconst workload=process.argv[2]||'target';\nconst runId=process.argv[3]||'matched';\nconst expectedRevision=await Bun.file(root+'/revisions.json').json();\nconst expectedBytes=new TextEncoder().encode(await Bun.file(dirs.baseline+'/bench/'+workload+'.json').json().then((rows:any[])=>'id,customer,total\\n'+rows.filter(r=>r.status==='open').map(r=>r.id+',\"'+r.customer.replaceAll('\"','\"\"')+'\",'+Number(r.total).toFixed(2)+'\\n').join('')));\nconst raw:any[]=[];\nfor(let round=-1;round<5;round++)for(const side of ['baseline','treatment'] as const){\n const dir=dirs[side];\n const actual=sha(dir);\n const dirty=Bun.spawnSync(['git','diff','--exit-code','HEAD','--','bench'],{cwd:dir}).exitCode!==0;\n const args=[process.execPath,'--no-install',dir+'/bench/export.ts',dir+'/bench/'+workload+'.json',root+'/runs/'+runId+'/'+round+'-'+side+'.csv'];\n const start=performance.now();\n const proc=Bun.spawnSync(args,{env:{...process.env,NODE_ENV:'production'}});\n const elapsedMs=performance.now()-start;\n const bytes=await Bun.file(args.at(-1)!).arrayBuffer();\n let counts:any; try{counts=JSON.parse(proc.stdout.toString());}catch{counts={errors:'unknown',completed:'unknown'};}\n raw.push({round,side,args,exit:proc.exitCode,stdout:proc.stdout.toString(),stderr:proc.stderr.toString(),elapsedMs,actualRevision:actual,expectedRevision:expectedRevision[side],dirty,revisionValid:!dirty&&actual===expectedRevision[side],workload,workloadSha256:hash(await Bun.file(dir+'/bench/'+workload+'.json').arrayBuffer()),outputSha256:hash(bytes),bytes:bytes.byteLength,outputCorrect:hash(bytes)===hash(expectedBytes),...counts});\n}\nconst summaries:any={};\nfor(const side of ['baseline','treatment']){const rows=raw.filter(r=>r.side===side&&r.round>=0),values=rows.map(r=>r.elapsedMs).sort((a,b)=>a-b);summaries[side]={samples:rows.length,medianMs:values[2],rangeMs:[values[0],values[4]]};}\nconst b=summaries.baseline,t=summaries.treatment,delta=b.medianMs-t.medianMs;\nconst gate=raw.every(r=>r.exit===0&&r.errors===0&&r.completed===20000&&r.outputCorrect&&r.revisionValid)&&new Set(raw.map(r=>r.workloadSha256)).size===1&&workload==='target';\nconst summary={runId,workload,environment:'NODE_ENV=production',concurrency:1,warmupsPerSide:1,timedRegion:'process start through awaited disk write and exit',...summaries,deltaMs:delta,percent:100*delta/b.medianMs,aboveObservedVariation:delta>Math.max(b.rangeMs[1]-b.rangeMs[0],t.rangeMs[1]-t.rangeMs[0]),gate};\nawait Bun.write(root+'/runs/'+runId+'/raw.json',JSON.stringify(raw,null,2));\nawait Bun.write(root+'/runs/'+runId+'/summary.json',JSON.stringify(summary,null,2));\nconsole.log(JSON.stringify(summary,null,2));\n"
    },

    {
      "path": "fixtures/cases/comparable-revisions/outputs/how-and-plan.md",
      "bytes": 2998,
      "sha256": "5bc0595bd7d3dde8aafae589c806f2bf0145e212ec58dc15b49d3fa2e81c343a",
      "owner": "local-013-initial-comparable-revisions",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "Overview. The root-prepared exporter reads JSON, validates every row, skips closed rows, formats each open total, quotes customer data, awaits one CSV disk write, and reports errors/completed rows. Three supplied orders generate fixed small, target and wide workloads. This is synthetic fixture relevance, not a production workload study.\n\nKey concepts. One CLI process owns an order array and accumulated CSV string. The timer includes process startup, JSON read/parse, formatting, CSV serialization, write completion and exit. The reference check consumes and hashes all CSV bytes outside the timed process.\n\nHow it works. Baseline bench/export.ts creates Intl.NumberFormat once per exported row. Separate baseline profile sampled format 146 times and NumberFormat 30 times out of 183 samples. This implicates number formatting on one CPU thread; it does not measure a complete production resource ceiling. The 14-core host load was 6.38/7.58/8.12, sides will alternate under contention. getconf observed 14 logical processors; sysctl probe was denied. OS filesystem cache remains uncontrolled and disclosed.\n\nWhere things live. Prepared baseline full SHA 92109959223ead77d476627b1087571ec6abe1c1 is the child of immutable c754fd2900108a70f7e730b2e057f5d5d80c6828, adding only four bench files. Frozen input bytes stay untouched. outputs/working/baseline is a separate local clone. Harness outputs/measure.ts adapts the root-prepared alternating harness with full Git pins and literal reference checks; source copy original-compare.ts preserved. A hash ArrayBuffer error aborted initial first sample and was repaired before freezing valid runs, failed return retained.\n\nGotchas. No actual control-cli companion exists under the only permitted package root. Native Bun process execution/profiling is an observed reduced CLI control binding. Matching-companion capture gate remains open, no full-host or production claim. Fresh-baseline-samples-repaired contains five alternating baseline-vs-identical-baseline samples per label; small apparent difference is within observed variation. No treatment planned before these baseline captures.\n\nHow exploration/explanation is parent sequential due bounded worker capacity; no independent how claim. One module and one top-level loop is the simple route. Implementation now has a separate fresh worker. No function boundary is crossed by hoisting a formatter constant inside the same module, so architect not triggered. Source architect and runner/rationale/red-flags were read but no architecture-workflow claim follows.\n\nMantras in exact order. 1 Don't do it: exported rows and exact bytes are required, so no work can be dropped. 2 Do it, but don't do it again: reuse one formatter for all exported rows. This is the first candidate. 3 Do it less, 4 Do it later, 5 Do it when they're not looking, 6 Do it concurrently, 7 Do it cheaper remain untried if mantra 2 meets target. No Hillclimb attempt-floor or plateau rule replaces the one-off stop rule.\n"
    },

      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "# perf(export): reuse the total formatter\n\n## Why\n\nThe fixture exporter rebuilds its number formatter for every open order. Reuse one formatter with the same locale and options.\n\n## What changed\n\n- Hoist Intl.NumberFormat allocation out of the row loop.\n\n## Scope\n\nThis local draft covers the synthetic exporter only. Production deployment tuning and complete companion-controlled verification remain open.\n\n## Blast Radius\n\nThe change touches total formatting. Literal CSV and error checks protect output behavior.\n\n## Verification\n\n- Local median export latency: 264.935 ms → 22.859 ms. Details and limits are in [comparison.json](comparison.json).\n- All target output bytes, completed rows and error counts match in [fresh matched samples](runs/matched/raw.json).\n\nDraft only. No PR created or readiness asserted. External actions are forbidden. Deslop is absent and no-comments independent review is unperformed.\n"

Keep comparable runs and their artifacts. A choice between fixes is not the checklist's one-run ballpark exception.\\n2. Run [how](../../how/SKILL.md) to ground hypotheses. Don't claim a perf ceiling without running it first.\\n   Try the performance mantras in order, cheapest first:\\n   1. Don't do it. Stop work whose result nothing uses rather than cheapening it.\\n   2. Do it, but don't do it again.\\n   3. Do it less.\\n   4. Do it later.\\n   5. Do it when they're not looking.\\n   6. Do it concurrently.\\n   7. Do it cheaper.\\n\\n   When an earlier mantra meets the target, stop.\\n3. Plan the fix from the trace. Keep profiling runs separate from reported timings. Flag it. Missing limiter, an untuned side, or unchecked errors or completed work makes a claimed difference inconclusive.
